### Assistant Response 1

> MySQL is up on 3307; pulling creature levels, item levels and stats from the
> world database now.

> Pulling boss stats and loot item levels per dungeon, and the same for the
> vanilla raids.

The stock loot already meets your goal. The upper Outland dungeons (Sethekk
Halls, Steamvault, Shattered Halls) drop blues that carry more stats than
Ahn'Qiraj 40 epics. The problem with a flat +4 is tanking. Once a creature is 4
or more levels above you, it can land **crushing blows** (150% damage), and the
accuracy cap patch doesn't limit those. From Blood Furnace up, almost every hit
that gets past the tank's dodge, parry and block is a crush.

Nothing is edited yet. All numbers come from queries against the vanilla world
database (its Outland dungeon creatures and loot are unmodified), using the
server's own health and damage formulas.

## 1. Levels and gear

"Budget ≈ epic ilvl" converts a blue's item level into the epic item level
with the same total stats. I used the commonly cited budget curves: blue ≈
(ilvl−1.84)/1.6, epic ≈ (ilvl−1.3)/1.3. Treat that column as approximate.

| Dungeon | Trash lvl (stock → +4) | Boss lvl (stock → +4) | Boss blue ilvl | Budget ≈ epic ilvl | Closest vanilla tier |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Hellfire Ramparts | 59–61 → 63–65 | 62 → 66 | 85 | ~69 | Molten Core / Zul'Gurub |
| Blood Furnace | 61–62 → 65–66 | 62–63 → 66–67 | 88 | ~71 | Molten Core |
| Slave Pens | 61–64 → 65–68 | 63–64 → 67–68 | 91 | ~74 | Blackwing Lair (lower) |
| Underbog | 62–65 → 66–69 | 65 → 69 | 94 | ~76 | Blackwing Lair |
| Mana-Tombs | 64–66 → 68–70 | 66 → 70 | 97 | ~79 | Ahn'Qiraj 40 (average) |
| Auchenai Crypts | 65–67 → 69–71 | 66–67 → 70–71 | 100 | ~81 | Ahn'Qiraj 40 (upper) |
| Sethekk Halls | 66–69 → 70–73 | 68 → 72 | 112–115 | ~91–93 | above Ahn'Qiraj 40 |
| Steamvault | 70 → 74 | 72 → 76 | 112–115 | ~91–93 | above Ahn'Qiraj 40 |
| Shattered Halls | 69–71 → 73–75 | 71–72 → 75–76 | 112–115 | ~91–93 | above Ahn'Qiraj 40 |

Vanilla gear with a level-60 requirement, from the same database:

| Source | Gear ilvl |
|---|---|
| Level-60 dungeons (Blackrock Depths and Spire, Scholomance, Stratholme) | blues 52–63 |
| Zul'Gurub | epics 65–68, blues 68–71 |
| Ahn'Qiraj 20 | epics 66–72, blues 71–74 |
| Molten Core | epics 61–71 (average 66) |
| Blackwing Lair | epics 73–77 |
| Ahn'Qiraj 40 | epics 73–88 (average 78) |

Naxxramas and Onyxia in this client are the level-80 versions, so Ahn'Qiraj 40
is the top vanilla tier a level 60 can reach.

The Outland gear is stronger at level 60 than its item level suggests. Burning
Crusade gear uses ratings (crit rating, hit rating), and a rating point is worth
more at lower levels. It takes about 14 crit rating for 1% crit at 60, against
about 22 at 70, so every rating stat on that gear is about 1.6× as strong on a
level 60.

## 2. Boss health, damage and time to kill

**Health and damage.** Raising a creature's level changes its health, its melee
damage, its armor, and whether it can crush. Boss spell damage mostly comes from
the spell's own fixed values, so +4 barely changes it. Ramparts and Blood
Furnace bosses get about 15–30% more health. At the other end, Shattered Halls
and Steamvault bosses get about 12% more health, but land crushing blows on
every hit that isn't avoided.

**Assumptions.** I haven't measured any of these on this server:
- each damage dealer does 600 DPS and each tank 250;
- a 5-player group is 3 damage dealers and 1 tank (2,050 DPS);
- a 40-player raid is 25 damage dealers and 5 tanks (16,250 DPS);
- each healer heals 700 per second;
- the tank has 9,000 armor and avoids 40% of attacks.

The "Tank damage/s" column counts only boss melee after armor and crushes, with
no boss spells.

| Boss | Level | Health | Melee DPS | Time to kill | Crush chance | Tank damage/s | Healers needed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Omor (Ramparts) | 66 | 68k | 820 | 33 s | 45% | 332 | 0.5 |
| Keli'dan (Blood Furnace) | 67 | 46k | 625 | 22 s | 55% | 276 | 0.4 |
| Quagmirran (Slave Pens) | 68 | 118k | 1,112 | 57 s | 65% | 517 | 0.7 |
| Black Stalker (Underbog) | 69 | 87k | 1,296 | 42 s | 75% | 616 | 0.9 |
| Shaffar (Mana-Tombs) | 70 | 101k | 848 | 49 s | 85% | 412 | 0.6 |
| Exarch Maladaar (Auchenai Crypts) | 71 | 95k | 2,012 | 46 s | 95% | **997** | **1.4** |
| Ikiss (Sethekk Halls) | 72 | 88k | 1,202 | 43 s | 105% | 607 | 0.9 |
| Kalithresh (Steamvault) | 76 | 165k | 1,208 | 80 s | 145% | 650 | 0.9 |
| Kargath (Shattered Halls) | 76 | 188k | 1,115 | **92 s** | 145% | 600 | 0.9 |
| *Hakkar (Zul'Gurub)* | 63 | 720k | 1,188 | 44 s | 0 | 319 | 0.5 |
| *Golemagg (Molten Core)* | 63 | 826k | 1,277 | 51 s | 0 | 343 | 0.5 |
| *Chromaggus (Blackwing Lair)* | 63 | 1.83M | 1,916 | 113 s | 0 | 514 | 0.7 |
| *Huhuran (Ahn'Qiraj 40)* | 63 | 1.0M | 1,597 | 61 s | 0 | 429 | 0.6 |
| *Twin Emperor Vek'nilash (Ahn'Qiraj 40)* | 63 | 2.17M | 1,916 | 133 s | 0 | 514 | 0.7 |

A crush chance above 100% is only possible because the chance keeps rising with
the level gap. In practice, every hit that isn't avoided lands as a crush.

What the numbers say:
- **Time to kill.** From Slave Pens up, a 5-player group needs about as long per
  boss as a 40-player raid needs for Molten Core or Ahn'Qiraj 40. Shattered
  Halls and Steamvault take longer than most raid bosses.
- **Healing.** On melee alone, a 5-player group with one healer is barely enough
  from Underbog up. Auchenai Crypts and anything with real spell damage go past
  one healer. A raid spreads that load over 8–10 healers. Raids get hard
  through boss mechanics, which +4 doesn't add.
- **Why the tank hurts.** Raid bosses are level 63. This server's crush rule
  only triggers at 4 or more levels, so raid bosses never crush. Every +4
  dungeon creature can, including trash. Armor also protects less against
  higher-level attackers: 9,000 armor blocks 55% of a level-63 attacker's damage
  but 40% of a level-76's.
- **What the accuracy cap patch leaves out.** It caps hit, miss, dodge and parry
  at a 3-level gap. It doesn't touch crushing blows, armor mitigation,
  level-based spell resists, or aggro radius, which grows with the level gap.
- **Vaelastrasz** is left out of the comparison. His script starts him at 30%
  health, so his template health overstates the fight.

## 3. Buddy bots: what changes in the design

- Seven buddies at 60 matches the buddy-bots plan: one at creation, plus one
  every 10 levels. Five players plus their buddies make a full 40-player raid.
- **Raids become the top tier again.** That conflicts with the stock loot.
  Sethekk, Steamvault and Shattered Halls blues (~91–93 by stat budget, plus
  the rating bonus) beat most Ahn'Qiraj 40 loot, which leaves raids pointless
  for gear.
- **"No regular bots" reverses current settings.** The basic profile's bot
  config file currently turns on the ambient random-bot population, levels
  1–60, across both continents and Outland. Turning it off leaves the world
  populated only by players and their buddies.
- **Buddy damage output is unknown.** Every time to kill above assumes competent
  players. How much damage the bot AI does, compared with a person, is the
  biggest gap in these numbers. A level-60 bot group in Ramparts with a damage
  logger would measure it, and that test is already the last step of the Outland
  dungeons issue.

## Open questions

1. **Crushing blows at +4.** From Blood Furnace up, nearly every hit on the tank
   that gets past dodge, parry and block is a crush. Is that the difficulty you
   want? The alternative is to switch crushing off for these creatures with a
   per-creature "no crushing blows" setting, so +4 only adds health, damage and
   armor.
2. **Raids against Outland gear.** Now that 5 players can raid, should Ahn'Qiraj
   40 be the top tier? If so, the three upper dungeons' loot needs to come down,
   or the raids need to come up.
3. **Random bots.** Should the basic profile's ambient random bots be switched
   off completely, leaving only buddies?
4. **Buddies in raids.** When a player enters a raid, do all 7 of their buddies
   join? The buddy plan so far only covers filling a 5-player dungeon party.
5. **Recording the +4 decision.** Can I write your answer into the Outland
   dungeons issue and change its level-64 rule to +4 per creature?

Starting with the first: is a level-76 boss that crushes on every hit the
difficulty you want, or should +4 only change health, damage and armor?

The player invites them manually so they can choose. If the player enters a
dungeon, the group is randomly created. If they have a party, then the remaining
slots are filled in the dungeon. If a player has a group with another player, or
two players, etc, then the bots who fill the rest of the group are chosen
randomly, to fit their group requirements. (to fit their group requirements
might be out of scope, and that's okay, we might just turn off the auto-fill if
there are more than one human players in a group.) - bots don't join other
players group, like for example if there's two humans exploring a particular
area and someone invites a bot that's not "theirs", then they'll send a message
saying "sorry, I don't follow other masters" or something similar.

### User Request 5

Base directory for this skill: /home/ritz/.claude/skills/issue-lifecycle

# Issue lifecycle

The owner's CLAUDE.md is the authority on what issue files are for and what
they must contain; where it and this skill ever disagree, CLAUDE.md wins. This
skill adds the mechanics CLAUDE.md leaves open: which tools answer which
question, in what order, and what to do when a tool says no.

All tools live in one place and are called by absolute path:

| Tool | Answers |
|---|---|
| `/home/ritz/programming/ai-stuff/scripts/validate-issues <project>` | is the issue tree consistent; next free number per phase |
| `validate-issues <project> --next <phase>` | the number to give a new issue |
| `validate-issues <project> --file <issue>` | is this one issue well-formed and are its links matched |
| `/home/ritz/programming/ai-stuff/scripts/progress-dashboard.lua <project> -m` | done/open counts per phase (for progress files and demos) |
| `/home/ritz/programming/ai-stuff/scripts/commit-own-changes <repo> -F -` | commits exactly the lines this session wrote, plus the changed transcripts of the commit's projects (any conversation's), and runs the repository's commit hooks, without touching the shared staging area |
| `/home/ritz/programming/ai-stuff/scripts/stage-own-changes <repo>` | previews what that commit would take; writes nothing |
| `/home/ritz/programming/ai-stuff/scripts/claim-own-change <file>` | records lines this session changed by a route the edit ledger cannot see (see Committing) |

Each tool has a `.info.md` beside it; read that rather than the source.

## Before writing a new issue

1. **Search first, including `issues/completed/`.** Grep the issue tree for
   the feature's nouns, not just the likely title. A completed issue about the
   same machinery is reopened and extended rather than duplicated: history is
   more useful stacked vertically in one file than spread across several.
2. **Pick the phase by what the work builds on**, not by when it is being
   done. Lower numbers are foundations; later issues depend on earlier ones.
3. **Take the number from the tool:** `validate-issues <project> --next
   <phase>`.
   It reads the project's own naming shape (522, 1001, 9-007, A04) and never
   reuses a number that a completed file already holds.
4. **Write the blueprint** with the three sections CLAUDE.md requires, plus
   link fields in the shape the project already uses (look at a neighbour).
   Name related functions, structures and files instead of pasting code.
5. **Check it:** `validate-issues <project> --file <new-issue>`, and fix what it
   reports on the new file. If the new issue blocks or is blocked by others,
   add the matching line to the other side too.

## While working

- Keep **Current Behavior** true. It is the only section that changes while
  an issue is in progress; rewrite it in place rather than appending a log.
- A question that surfaces is written into the issue (an "Open questions"
  section) and then asked. An issue with an unanswered question is in
  progress, not done.
- Anything discovered that changes another issue's assumptions is written into
  that issue now, not at completion.

## Splitting into sub-issues

Split when an issue holds work streams that could be built, tested or reviewed
separately, or when it cannot be finished in one sitting. Do not split an issue
that is already one mechanism. When splitting, produce and then write out:

1. **A table** — `| ID | Name | Dependencies | Description |`, where ID is the
   parent number plus a lower-case letter (103a, 103b), Name is dash-separated
   lower-case words, Dependencies is "None" or sibling IDs.
2. **The rationale** — the distinct work streams, why one issue cannot hold
   them, what splitting buys.
3. **The execution order** — a small dependency graph, e.g.
   `103a (foundation) → 103b (needs 103a) → 103c (parallel with 103b)`.

Each row becomes a file `{parent}{letter}-{name}.md` with the three sections.
The parent keeps its blueprint and gains a short list of its sub-issues; the
analysis itself is not appended to the parent (that would be worklog, not
blueprint). Run `validate-issues` afterwards: it reports orphaned sub-issues
and one-sided links.

## Completing an issue

An issue is complete only when nothing in it is deferred and no open question
is unanswered. Then, in this order:

1. **Run the project's tests.** A fixed bug gets a test that would have caught
   it.
2. **Rewrite the issue as the blueprint of what was built**: Current Behavior
   states the built system; steps name the real functions and files; decisions
   not taken are stated with their reason. Poetry the owner wrote during the
   work goes into the issue verbatim.
3. **Move it** into `issues/completed/` (`git mv`, so the history follows).
4. **Update `issues/phase-<N>-progress.md`** for that phase. Take counts from
   `progress-dashboard.lua <project> -m`, or better, name the command instead
   of copying numbers that will go stale.
5. **Update related issues** whose Current Behavior or assumptions changed.
6. **Run `validate-issues <project>`** and fix new findings that the change
   caused. Findings older than the change are reported to the owner, not
   silently fixed in passing.
7. **Commit** as below.

## Committing

The rule (CLAUDE.md): a commit carries exactly the lines this session wrote —
never another session's work, even if it sits in the same file — and every
changed transcript of the commit's projects rides along — the session's own
project folder and the projects of the files it commits — this
conversation's and any other's left behind (a session that committed, talked
on and quit), so each project's story in git stays whole.

Commit small and often: each piece as soon as it is done and checked, not a
pile at the end.

1. Optionally preview: `stage-own-changes <repo>` lists what would be taken
   and what left out, and writes nothing.
2. Commit: `commit-own-changes <repo> -F -` with the message on standard
   input. It builds the commit on a private staging list from this session's
   own lines (the edit ledger the edit hook keeps) plus those transcripts, and
   never touches the shared staging area, so nothing another
   session has staged can ride along. Plain `git commit` is refused by the
   commit gate.
3. Message style: say what the software now does, in plain English and by
   mechanism or analogy; mention any extra changes and why. No function names.

**When it stops or leaves something out.**

- **A tangled ("mixed") block** means another session changed lines right
  next to or on top of yours. Nothing is committed. Tell the owner which file
  and line; decide together what that region should say. `--leave-mixed`
  commits your other blocks meanwhile.
- **Lines that are yours but were written by a route the ledger could not see**
  (a script you ran, a generator, `sed`, a rename): record them with
  `claim-own-change <file>` and say so in the commit message.
- **A warning about the shared staging area** means someone had staged that
  file by hand in a way that overlaps your commit; their entry was left alone.
  Tell the owner.

The one-time permission tokens (`/tmp/claude-allow-*`) belong to the person.
Never create one; if a gate names a token, relay the gate's message and let
the owner decide.

## Open questions

- Phase ≥ 10 in compact names depends on the project's issue-number width;
  `validate-issues` decides it from evidence (progress files, `phase-N-demo`
  names). A project with none of those gets a warning. Should the house rule
  fix one width (two digits, as in the 522 example) for every new project?

I've answered each question below and written your decisions into the issue
files. Nothing is built or committed yet.

## Can we change the damage and healing of creature spells?

Yes. The server makes a script call on every hit, spell, damage-over-time tick
and heal, and the script can change the amount. There is one call for each:
- melee hits
- direct spell hits
- damage-over-time ticks
- heals

Basic already has its own compiled rules file, where the talent cap lives, so
the multipliers can go there. The plan is a table of multipliers per dungeon:
creature melee, creature spells, healing on players, and creature health. That's
a new sub-issue under the basic profile.

This matters more than levels do. A boss spell's damage is a fixed number in the
spell's own data, so +4 levels barely touches it. The same table is how
Naxxramas's level-80 spells get brought down to level-60 strength.

## How armor and spell resists depend on level

Both come from the server's combat code.

**Armor.** Your damage reduction from armor is armor ÷ (armor + a constant).
The constant is set by the **attacker's** level, and above 59 it grows about
5.5× faster than below.
- Against a level-63 attacker the constant is 7,285, so 9,000 armor stops 55%.
- Against a level-76 attacker it's 13,362, so the same armor stops 40%.

Separately, a creature's own armor comes from a per-level table, so a
higher-level creature also takes less from your physical attacks. Capping that
part would take a separate armor multiplier in the new table.

**Spell resists.** These are partial resists: part of each spell hit is shrugged
off, in 10% steps.
- The target gains 5 resistance for every level it is above the caster.
- The resistance also has to be compared against a constant, and that constant
  grows with the **target's** own level.
- For a level-60 caster, the average amount resisted is about 11% on a level 63,
  13% on a 64, and 22% on a 76.

**Aggro radius.** A creature notices you from 20 yards, plus 1 yard for every
level it is above you. A level 76 notices a level 60 from 36 yards. Basic
doesn't carry the patch that reverses this.

The plan is to hold all three at a 3-level gap, the way hits and misses already
are. It's written into the accuracy-cap issue.

## Gear usable at 60 that drops in the open world

The item-level ranges come from the database. The "≈ vanilla epic" column is
the same rough stat-budget conversion as last time. The time-to-kill column
assumes 600 DPS per damage dealer, 2,050 for a group of five and 16,250 for 40.

| Source | Gear (item level) | ≈ vanilla epic | Farmed from | Health | Time to kill |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Azeroth normal mobs, 55–63 | greens/blues 59–65, rare epics 60–72 | pre-raid | ~3,400–3,900 health | | ~6 s solo |
| Azeroth elites, 60–63 | same pool | pre-raid | ~45,000 health | | ~22 s for 5 |
| Emerald Dragons and Azuregos (63) | epics 71–72 | Molten Core / Zul'Gurub | 833,000–916,000 health | | ~55 s for 40 (≈ Molten Core bosses) |
| Outland normal mobs, 60–70 | greens 102–120 (required 64–70), blues 88–109 (required 61–68) | greens ~70–74, blues ~70–86 | ~4,800–8,300 health | | ~8–14 s solo |
| Outland elites, 67–70 | same pool | same | ~48,000 health | | ~24 s for 5 |
| Doomwalker and Doom Lord Kazzak (73) | epics 120 (required 70) | well above Naxxramas-40 | 0.85–1.6 million health, melee 5,600–11,000 per second | | a level-80-era fight |

Only 8 items dropped on Outland's open-world level-61/62 creatures are wearable
at 60 as-is (blues, item level 82–85). Everything else Outland drops requires
61 or higher.

## Onyxia and Naxxramas item levels

- **In this client** both are the level-80 versions. Naxxramas drops item level
  200 (10-player) and 213 (25-player); Onyxia drops 232 and 245.
- **Original vanilla:** Onyxia's loot was about 71–76, roughly Blackwing Lair.
  Naxxramas-40's was 83–92: tier 3 at 88, the weapons and Atiesh at 89–92.
- The vanilla items are still in the item table, but nothing drops them.
- The database doesn't record which vanilla boss dropped which item. Restoring
  the loot needs an outside source, or your own list.

## Dungeons a level 60 can enter unmodified

- Every vanilla instance. Blackwing Lair needs 60; the rest need 50 or less.
- The nine Outland dungeons in the table: Ramparts, Blood Furnace, Shattered
  Halls, Slave Pens, Underbog, Steamvault, Mana-Tombs, Auchenai Crypts and
  Sethekk Halls. All need 55.

Next by entry level:
- Old Hillsbrad (64)
- Shadow Labyrinth, Magisters' Terrace and Utgarde Keep (65)
- Black Morass and The Nexus (66)
- Botanica, Mechanar and Azjol-Nerub (67)
- Arcatraz and Karazhan (68)

Utgarde Keep, The Nexus and Azjol-Nerub are Northrend dungeons, and Karazhan is
a raid.

## What the gear ladder would look like

Every normal-mode Outland dungeon's blues top out at item level 115, which is
about a Naxxramas-40 epic. So the ladder fills in by itself:

| Vanilla tier (epic item level) | Outland match |
|---|---|
| Zul'Gurub / Molten Core / Onyxia (66–71) | Ramparts, Blood Furnace, Outland open-world greens |
| Blackwing Lair / Ahn'Qiraj 20 (73–77) | Slave Pens, Underbog |
| Ahn'Qiraj 40 (78–81) | Mana-Tombs, Auchenai Crypts |
| Naxxramas-40 (83–92) | Sethekk Halls, Steamvault, Shattered Halls; also, if opened: Old Hillsbrad, Shadow Labyrinth, Mechanar, Botanica, Arcatraz, Black Morass, Magisters' Terrace |

Doing this means three changes:
1. Lower the entry level to 60 for the seven extra dungeons.
2. Set their gear's required level to 60, as already done for the first nine.
3. Keep Karazhan and the heroic modes closed. Their epics are item level 115+
   and would sit well above Naxxramas-40.

With +4, the seven added dungeons' bosses become level 74–76, like Shattered
Halls. That's where the new damage multipliers do the tuning.

There are two ways this overshoots:

### User Request 7

Base directory for this skill:
/tmp/claude-1000/bundled-skills/2.1.280/f4870062347979b09b9f4f5f8b4c97eb/dataviz

# Data Visualization

A chart is **read by people and executed by you**. This skill turns "make it
look
good" into a procedure with checks, so the result is right by construction
rather
than by taste.

**The method here is design-system-agnostic.** Nothing in the procedure, the
form
heuristic, the six checks, or the mark specs is specific to one product. A
design
system supplies a small set of *parameters* (its ramps, a categorical order, a
diverging pair, a status palette, a texture, its surfaces, its filter
components);
the method consumes them unchanged. A **validated default palette** is the
reference instance, fully specified in `references/palette.md`. To target your
brand, read that file's structure and substitute its values - touch nothing
else.

> The single most important habit: **the color part is computable, so compute
> it.**
> Never eyeball whether a palette is colorblind-safe - run
> `scripts/validate_palette.js`.

## The procedure - do these in order

Color comes LAST. Most bad charts pick colors first.

1. **Pick the form.** What is the data's job - magnitude, identity, polarity, a
   single headline, change-over-time? The job picks the chart type, and
   sometimes
   the answer is *not a chart* (a stat tile or hero number). ->
   `references/choosing-a-form.md`
2. **Assign color by the job it does.** Categorical (identity), sequential
   (magnitude), diverging (polarity), or status (state) - each has one rule.
   Assign categorical hues in fixed order, never cycled. ->
   `references/color-formula.md`
3. **VALIDATE the palette - run the script, don't reason about Delta E.**
   `node scripts/validate_palette.js "<hex,hex,...>" --mode light` (relative to
   this skill's base directory - or load it as `<script type="module">` in the
   chart's own page, where it reads
   `data-palette` off `<body>` and logs a `console.table` report). It returns
   pass/fail on the lightness band, chroma floor, adjacent-pair CVD separation,
   the normal-vision floor, and contrast. Fix anything that FAILs before
   continuing. Re-run for
   `--mode dark` with that mode's surface.
4. **Apply mark specs & spacers.** Thin marks, 4px rounded data-ends anchored to
   the baseline, 2px lines, >=8px markers, a 2px surface gap between fills
   (stacked
   segments and adjacent bars alike) and a 2px surface ring on overlapping
   marks,
   selective direct labels. -> `references/marks-and-anatomy.md`
5. **Add the hover layer - by default.** An HTML/SVG chart *is* interactive;
   ship
   a crosshair+tooltip on line/area and a per-mark hover tooltip on
   bar/dot/cell.
   The only form that skips it is a bare stat tile with no plot. Hit targets
   bigger
   than the mark; filters in one row above the charts. ->
   `references/interaction.md`
6. **Final accessibility pass.** For >= 2 series a legend is always present and
   <= 4
   are also direct-labeled (a single series needs no legend box - the title
   names
   it), so identity is never color-alone; a table view exists; dark mode is
   **selected** - its own
   steps from the same ramps, validated against the dark surface, not an
   automatic
   flip; texture is available for the CVD/print/forced-colors case.
7. **Render it and look at it.** The validator checks color, not layout - open
   or
   screenshot the output and eyeball it for label collisions, geometry, and
   overflow
   before calling it done.

Then check the result against **`references/anti-patterns.md`** - it is the
catalog
of what goes wrong. If your chart matches an entry, it's wrong.

## Non-negotiables (true in every design system)

- **Assign categorical hues in fixed order, never cycled.** A 9th series is
  never a
  generated hue - it folds into "Other," small multiples, or composite encoding.
- **One axis.** Never a dual-axis chart (two y-scales). Two measures of
  different
  scale -> two charts, small multiples, or indexed to a common base. *(This is
  the
  #1 chart mistake - see anti-patterns.)*
- **Color follows the entity, never its rank.** A filter that changes the series
  count must not repaint the survivors.
- **Sequential = one hue, light->dark. Diverging = two hues + a neutral gray
  midpoint.** Never a rainbow; never a hue at the diverging midpoint.
- **Run the validator before shipping any categorical palette.** CVD Delta E >=
  8 is the
  target (OKLab ×100); 6-8 is a floor that is legal ONLY with secondary
  encoding. A
  normal-vision floor below 15 is a hard FAIL - full-color readers can't tell
  the
  pair apart; re-step it on the adjacent pairlist (secondary encoding does not
  excuse
  this one); under `--pairs all` cut series or facet instead - see check 4. A
  contrast WARN
  obligates visible labels or a table view - it is not dismissable.
- **Thin marks; a legend always present for >= 2 series (none for one), with
  selective direct labels (never a number on every point); recessive
  grid/axes.**
- **Text wears text tokens, never the series color** - values, labels, and
  legends
  stay in primary/secondary/muted ink; a colored mark beside them carries
  identity.
- **Status colors are reserved** (good/warning/serious/critical) and never
  reused
  for "series 4"; they ship with an icon + label, never color alone.

## Plugging in a design system

The method is invariant; only these parameters change per system. The reference
instance - every value filled in - is `references/palette.md`.

| Parameter | What the system provides |
|---|---|
| **Ramps** | the hue scales (named steps) the palette draws from |
| **Categorical theme** | the fixed hue order (a named theme); default + alternates |
| **Sequential hue** | the default single hue for magnitude |
| **Diverging pair** | two warm/cool poles + a neutral midpoint |
| **Status palette** | good / warning / serious / critical - steps distinct from categorical |
| **Texture fill** | one directional hand-drawn fill, used at 45° / 135° |
| **Surfaces** | light & dark chart-surface colors (the validator needs these) |
| **Filter controls** | date-range & dimension controls (behavioral spec in `interaction.md`) |

To onboard a new system: fill those rows, feed its ramps to the validator, and
let
it snap each slot to the nearest passing step. Structure and rules stay as
written.

## Reference files

| File | What it answers |
|------|-----------------|
| `references/choosing-a-form.md` | Which chart type / is it even a chart? |
| `references/color-formula.md` | The four jobs, the six checks, snap-to-passing |
| `references/marks-and-anatomy.md` | Mark specs, spacers, labels, figures, hero number |
| `references/interaction.md` | Tooltips & hover, filters & time ranges |
| `references/components.md` | The pieces a chart is made of - build each in plain HTML |
| `references/anti-patterns.md` | **What goes wrong - check every chart against this** |
| `references/palette.md` | **The reference palette instance** - every parameter, filled in; swap for your brand's |
| `scripts/validate_palette.js` | Runnable six-checks validator (run it; don't eyeball) |

> Building the comparison chart; pulling a few more exact ranges first.

I've recorded all your answers in the issue files, and the chart comparing the
three gear sources is here: https://claude.ai/artifact/MZ1mumH84gVxVicSnskvD8.
It puts vanilla raids, the +4 Outland dungeons and each Outland zone's drops on
one scale, with a toggle between stat budget and raw item level. Hovering a row
shades its range across every other row, so you can line tiers up. Nothing is
built or committed yet, because no issue is finished.

## Your questions

**Ratings against stronger enemies.** A rating turns into a percentage using the
wearer's level, never the enemy's. So Burning Crusade gear keeps its ~1.6×
bonus on a level 60 against any creature. What the enemy's level changes is its
own health, damage and armor. Per creature level, using the server's per-level
base stats:
- health goes up about 3.2%;
- weapon damage goes up about 5.1%;
- armor goes up about 5.2%.

From the Ramparts bosses (level 66 after +4) to the Shattered Halls bosses (76),
creatures gain 35% health and 46% base damage. Over the same climb the gear
gains about 37% in stat budget (blue item level 85 → 115). The enemies keep
pace with the gear and pull slightly ahead, so each tier needs the previous
tier's gear and the top stays hard. That matches "possible, but not easy".

**"Exponential" effects.** None of the level effects is actually exponential:
- **Armor** is linear.
- **Spell resists** peak around 22% near level 76, then fall off. The resistance
  a creature gains per level is linear, but the number it's divided by grows
  with the square of the creature's level.
- **Crushing blows** were the only steep one: from none to every landed hit
  within about four levels. Your "no crushes from level 64 up" removes it. Below
  64 crushing still works as stock, so a level-45 creature can still crush a
  level-40 player.

I kept armor and resists stock and kept the aggro-radius cap you asked for
earlier.

**Attunements.** In normal mode only two dungeons are gated. Old Hillsbrad needs
the quest "The Caverns of Time", and Black Morass needs "Return to Andormu".
Both quests need level 66. Arcatraz and Shattered Halls need no key in normal
mode on this client. Heroic-mode requirements stay.

**Magisters' Terrace.** Yes, it's from the Sunwell patch. Its normal-mode blues
are item level 115, the same as the other normal dungeons, so it needs no
special handling. The one difference is that Kael'thas drops six item-level-110
epics, above the 100 that other dungeons' rare epics reach. They're on the
chart, and whether to treat them like the other epics is an open question.

**Doomwalker and Kazzak.** Both are world bosses of the same era: level 73, one
spawn each, each dropping from a pool of 10 item-level-120 epics. Both have real
fight mechanics:
- **Kazzak** marks a player and drains their mana until the mark explodes. He
  also heals himself by hurting that marked player, gets stronger whenever he
  kills someone, and has an enrage timer. He drops 2 items per kill.
- **Doomwalker** casts Earthquake and Chain Lightning, charges and knocks
  players down, and enrages below 20% health. Anyone he kills is marked, and he
  kills a marked player instantly if they come back.

**Fel Reaver.** You remembered right: no mechanics. It's an ordinary level-70
elite patrolling Hellfire Peninsula, with about 105,000 health, about 1,000
melee damage per second, and the normal level-70 loot table. Turning it into a
Patchwerk-style gear check would mean raising its health and damage per creature
and giving it its own loot.

**The pool for Kazzak.** Raid items of about his item level on this client come
from Karazhan (115–125), Gruul's Lair (125) and Magtheridon's Lair (125). On
the chart that pool sits above everything else.

## What the chart shows about zone gear

The stock loot already rises from zone to zone. The table uses the middle half
of what each zone's normal creatures drop; "≈ epic" is the stat-budget
conversion used on the chart:

| Zone | Greens | ≈ epic | Rare blues | ≈ epic |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Hellfire Peninsula | 84–92 | 53–58 | 88–91 | 71–74 |
| Zangarmarsh | 92–95 | 58–60 | 91–97 | 74–79 |
| Terokkar Forest | 95–104 | 60–66 | 97–106 | 79–86 |
| Nagrand | 98–105 | 62–67 | 100–108 | 81–88 |
| Blade's Edge | 102–107 | 65–68 | 105–109 | 85–88 |
| Netherstorm | 108–111 | 69–71 | 111–112 | 90–91 |
| Shadowmoon Valley | 110–114 | 70–73 | 111–114 | 90–92 |

Stock, the top zones' greens sit at about Molten Core / Zul'Gurub / Onyxia
epics, and their rare blues match Naxxramas-40 and the top dungeons. The zones
may need only their required levels lowered to 60, not new item levels.

## Issue files updated

- **Outland dungeons:** your answers, the seven extra dungeons opened with the
  two attunements removed, the item-level-100 epics set to required level 60,
  and the ratings explanation.
- **Accuracy cap:** no crushing blows from level 64 up and the aggro radius
  capped. Armor and resists stay stock.
- **Onyxia and Naxxramas:** loot from Wowhead or similar, the Wrath scripts kept
  and only rescaled, 10- and 25-player modes, and Onyxia's head quest and tier-2
  helm restored.
- **Damage and healing multipliers:** set per creature, and creatures healing
  themselves are scaled too.
- **Basic's bot config:** the auction house and battlegrounds are handled by
  buddies.
- **New:** buddies at the auction house.
- **New:** buddies in battlegrounds.
- **New:** Kazzak as the weekly world boss, including his mechanics, the loot
  pool, the contribution rules and the Doomwalker and Fel Reaver questions.
- **New:** the Outland world gear ladder, with the zone table and a link to the
  chart.

## Open questions, in the order I'd take them

1. Which vanilla tier should the top zones' greens match? Stock is about Molten
   Core. Should their rare blues be lowered so the dungeons stay ahead of
   farming?
2. Accept the rating overshoot? My analysis above says it's what lets gear keep
   pace with the +4 creatures.
3. Should the six item-level-110 Kael'thas epics also become wearable at 60?
4. Should the aggro-radius cap and the no-crushing rule also apply on beta,
   which shares the accuracy-cap patch?
5. **Kazzak:** how should contribution be weighted (damage against healing
   against damage taken)? How many items per kill? Which weekly reset time?
6. **Doomwalker:** a second weekly boss, or left stock? **Fel Reaver:** made
   into a gear-check boss, and at which tier?
7. **Onyxia and Naxxramas:** how many items per kill in 10- and 25-player mode?
   For tier 3, restore the vanilla token-and-quest path or drop the pieces
   directly?
8. Should players' healing inside the tuned dungeons also be scaled down?
9. **Buddies at the auction house:** how are prices set, how long do auctions
   run, and does money ever reach the owner?
10. **Buddies in battlegrounds:** on a small server the queues may never fill.
    Should other players' buddies fill the other side?
11. **Outland world gear:** should all of it become wearable at 60, or only
    creature drops, not quest rewards? Should the item-level-100 world epics
    drop everywhere or only in the top zones?
12. Does the bot module need random bots to start up at all? I still have to
    check this before switching them off.

Starting with the first: in Netherstorm and Shadowmoon Valley, which vanilla
tier should the greens match, and should the rare blues sit below the top
dungeons?

## Answers to your questions

**Setting ratings to about 1×.** I'd do it. The server converts ratings using
the character's own level, so on basic every character below 70 would be
converted at the level-70 rate. What that changes:
- The rating stats on Burning Crusade gear (crit, hit, haste, expertise,
  defense, dodge, parry, block) lose about 37%. Stamina, strength, intellect,
  spell power and attack power don't change.
- Vanilla gear doesn't change, because its crit and hit come as "equip:"
  effects, not ratings. That quietly helps the raids and dungeons sit at the
  same tier.
- Your worry is right, and 1× shrinks the gap. In this client, heal-over-time
  spells never crit, most damage-over-time spells don't, and haste doesn't add
  extra ticks. So a 1.6× rating bonus mostly helps crit- and haste-stacking
  specs.
- The hit cap and the defense needed to stop being crit take more gear to reach.
- The character sheet's percentages come from the server and stay correct. The
  client's own rating tooltips would show the old numbers unless the client's
  rating table is patched too.

**Kael'thas's level.** I suggest 78, two above the other Magisters' Terrace
bosses, as you guessed. His loot is a notch above the dungeon's blues, so a
notch more difficulty fits. Level matters less for him than for most bosses,
though. His melee is weak (about 700 per second) and his danger comes from his
spells, which level doesn't scale. His real difficulty will come from the
per-creature multipliers.

**Proposed ladder for the upper dungeons.** Sethekk Halls through Magisters'
Terrace would continue the step the lower six already take, about +2.4 on the
stat-budget scale per dungeon, ending at Naxxramas-40's range (≈ epic
83–92):

| Dungeon | Boss level now (+4) | Proposed | Blues now (≈ epic) | Proposed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Old Hillsbrad | not measured | 71 | not measured | 83 |
| Sethekk Halls | 72 | 72 | 91–93 | 84 |
| Black Morass | not measured | 72 | not measured | 85 |
| Shadow Labyrinth | 76 | 73 | 91–93 | 86 |
| Shattered Halls | 75–76 | 73 | 91–93 | 87 |
| Steamvault | 76 | 74 | 91–93 | 88 |
| Mechanar / Botanica / Arcatraz | 76 | 74 / 75 / 75 | 93 | 89 / 90 / 91 |
| Magisters' Terrace | 74–76 | 76 (Kael'thas 78) | 93 | 92 |

Loot would come down by scaling each item's stats to its target, not by swapping
items. That means each dungeon gets its own level offset instead of a flat +4.

**World blues, paired zone by zone with the dungeons:**

| Zone | Matches | Target (≈ epic) |
|---|---|---|
| Hellfire Peninsula | Hellfire Ramparts | 69 (≈ Ahn'Qiraj 20) |
| Zangarmarsh | Slave Pens / Underbog | 74–76 |
| Terokkar Forest | Mana-Tombs | 79 |
| Nagrand | Auchenai Crypts | 81 |
| Blade's Edge | between | 82 |
| Netherstorm, Shadowmoon Valley | top | 83–84 (≈ Ahn'Qiraj 40) |

**Fel Reaver.** Written up as you described. At stock values the health and
damage combinations work out as:

| Mechanics picked | Health | Melee per second |
|---|---|---|
| Both Kazzak | 850,000 | ~11,100 |
| Both Doomwalker | 1,590,000 | ~5,650 |
| One of each | 1,220,000 | ~8,390 |

Every one-and-one combination looks the same before the pull, as you wanted. On
basic these numbers come from the tuned Kazzak and Doomwalker rather than stock.

Two corrections from reading Kazzak's script:
- **His berserk is at 3 minutes, not 2**, and he also frenzies every 30 seconds
  after the first minute.
- **His mark only picks players who use mana**, so the stock version never lands
  on a warrior tank. The tank-swap rule is new behavior.

**Kazzak's items per kill.** Contributors × 2 ÷ 40 makes sense. It keeps each
player's expected share constant at 1/20th of an item per kill however many show
up. The fractional part becomes a chance, so 5 players get an item about one
kill in four.

**Auction house.** The bot module has no working auction behavior. Its only
auction code is commented out, and even that was a simulator: it made brand-new
copies of items at vendor price times a multiplier, instead of selling what a
bot carries. There's nothing to follow, so the buddy auction-house issue is now
the separate design issue you asked for.

**Battleground edge cases**, each written into the buddy battlegrounds issue and
needing a rule:
- The owner logs out while buddies are queued or mid-match.
- The owner queues together with another player.
- A buddy is in a match when its owner enters a dungeon.
- A buddy pulled out by an invite gets the stock Deserter debuff, which blocks
  its next queue.
- Arenas.

**Flights.** Every flight master on basic is already turned off, including
Outland's. Basic reuses vanilla's flight-path removal. So the change is to add
one flight: the Stair of Destiny flight master offers Honor Hold (Alliance) or
Thrallmar (Horde) straight away, with no need to discover the destination first.
About places that might need a flight:
- **Moonglade:** walkable through the Timbermaw Hold tunnel. I still need to
  check that the furbolgs let a new character through.
- **Tempest Keep's three dungeons, Skettis, Ogri'la, Netherwing Ledge:**
  reachable only by air. Flying (Expert Riding) is trainable at 60 on this
  client, and basic doesn't block it.
- **Isle of Quel'Danas (Magisters' Terrace):** reached by the portal in
  Shattrath. If that portal needs a quest or level 70, Magisters' Terrace
  becomes unreachable once quests are removed. This needs checking.

**Also recorded:** aggro radius stays stock. "No crushing from level 64 up"
becomes its own patch on basic's list, separate from the accuracy cap, so beta
can take it or leave it. Players' healing isn't scaled. Naxxramas and Onyxia
drop as many items as their Wrath versions and get the tier-3 token quests back.
No random bots on basic.

## Issue files updated

- **Outland dungeons:** the upper-dungeon ladder, Kael'thas, and the rating
  proposal.
- **Crushing-blow rule:** now its own patch; aggro radius, armor and resists
  stock.
- **Onyxia and Naxxramas:** items per kill and the tier-3 path.
- **Damage multipliers:** healing left alone.
- **Basic's bot config:** random bots off.
- **World bosses:** retitled for all three, with the Fel Reaver design and the
  Kazzak changes.
- **Outland world gear:** the zone pairing.
- **Buddies:** auction-house and battleground issues.
- **New:** Outland without quests.

You were right, and my earlier claim was wrong. In this client, vanilla gear's
"+1% crit" and "+2% hit" were turned into ratings, sized for level 60. So using
the level-70 rating rate for everyone would weaken vanilla gear by about 37%
too.

## Why vanilla gear carries ratings

I read the equip effects in the client's spell file:
- Band of Accuria's "+2% hit" is the spell "Increased Hit Rating 20".
- Onslaught Girdle's "+1% crit" is "Increased Critical 14".
- 282 of the 344 vanilla raid epics carry an equip effect like these.

At level 60, 14 crit rating is 1%, so vanilla gear is exactly on target. Only
Burning Crusade gear is oversized, because its ratings were sized for level 70.

**New proposal:** leave the rating rate alone. Reduce only the rating stats on
Burning Crusade items, to about 0.63× (14 ÷ 22.1), in the same stat-scaling
pass that already brings their budgets onto the ladder. Vanilla gear keeps its
intended values, Outland gear loses its 1.6× bonus, and the gap between HoT/DoT
specs and crit-stacking specs still narrows. The client tooltip caveat is noted
in the issue.

## Isle of Quel'Danas

- **The creatures don't shuffle in this database.** 1,001 of its 1,004 spawns
  are in the default phase. On the live servers the island's rebuilding phases
  changed vendors and NPCs, and the daily quests rotate, but the monsters stay
  put here.
- **How hard they are:** normal (not elite) blood elves, demons and naga, level
  69–71, about 5,400–7,200 health and 110–210 melee damage per second.
  That's on par with Netherstorm and Shadowmoon Valley. There are also some
  level-60 Greengill Slaves.
- **Loot:** greens item level 114–116 and blues 114. That puts it at the top
  of the open world, level with Netherstorm and Shadowmoon Valley. In the
  pairing I proposed, its blues come down to the Ahn'Qiraj 40 target (≈ epic
  83–84). It's now on the chart and in the Outland world gear issue.
- **Magisters' Terrace might not be a problem.** The Shattrath portal to the
  Isle has no condition, script or level requirement in the database. I haven't
  tried it in game yet.

**Flights to the Isle.** I read the client's flight network (the TaxiNodes,
TaxiPath and TaxiPathNode files) and measured each route along its waypoints:

| Flight master | Distance to the Isle |
|---|---|
| Silvermoon City | 5,523 yd |
| Zul'Aman | 7,526 yd |
| Tranquillien | 7,726 yd |
| **Light's Hope Chapel, Alliance** (closest Alliance master, direct route) | **13,772 yd** |
| **Light's Hope Chapel, Horde** | **14,007 yd** |
| Ironforge | 20,222 yd |

Both factions' closest match is the same chapel, so both fly from Light's Hope
and back, over nearly the same distance. Silvermoon, Zul'Aman and Tranquillien
get no flight to the Isle.

okay so... when buddies are in a town or city with an auction house, they should
build a small little task list to do while they're there, based on the
capabilities present in the town or city. This includes vendors, repairs,
auction houses, and trainers. Repairing is the highest priority if they have a
single red item, otherwise trainers are the highest. If they have no red items
but at least one yellow item, then repair is the 2nd highest priority behind
trainers. Otherwise repair is below auction house, which comes next. Then,
repair if they don't have any items that are damaged enough to be "yellow", and
vendoring trash goods at the final priority level after that. When at a repair
or vendor stop, they might buy items that are considered beneficial as well,
like food or potions or something, as long as we know that they will use these
items in the field. We should try and ensure that they do. We will update the
vendor lists later to include things like potions and such... Don't worry about
that now though. Once they're done with that, they'll walk to random NPCs chosen
either as "the next closest NPC in the general direction of either the most
distant NPC in the town, or the next closest NPC in the general direction of one
of their class trainers in the city" either that, or as just selecting a random
NPC in the town or city and walking to them. Make sure they path naturally to
the destination.

When they're at an auction house, they will sell greens, trade goods, and other
valuable items by first checking to see if there's any items that are higher
than their minimum price, which is 1.5x the vendor price. If so, for example if
a battleaxe is listed for 2x the vendor price, then they will undercut that
listing by 10% of it's cost if it's an equipment item, 5% if it's anything else.
They'll do 48hr listings for equipment, 12 hr for trade goods, and 24 hours for
everything else that a player might want or need. If there's only items at 1.5x
the vendor price they will either list theirs or just vendor them. For
equipment, they'll list it if there's another item of that type but a different
enchantment (of the tiger, of the whale, etc). The random enchantments are
considered unique items. If there's the exact item on the AH and there's at
least 5 of them, then they'll just vendor it unless it's an epic and then
they'll always post it. Quality adds .5x to the modifier, so greens are 1.5x
vendor price, whites are not auctioned at all, blue is 2x vendor price, and epic
is 2.5x vendor price. However if it's a level 60 or 60+ epic, they will never
auction them, and always mail them to their player instead. Any edge cases? The
money goes to the bot, not the player that they're following.

## Edge cases in the auction design

Each of these needs a rule:

1. **Soulbound items** can't be auctioned or mailed. A bind-on-pickup level-60
   epic therefore can't be mailed to the owner. Should the buddy equip it if
   it's an upgrade and otherwise vendor it?
2. **"Level 60 or 60+ epic"**: by required level or by item level? A Molten Core
   epic requires 60 but has item level 66.
3. **Only minimum-price listings present**: you said "either list theirs or just
   vendor them". What decides between the two, a coin flip or list when there
   are fewer than 5?
4. **Items with no vendor price** have no minimum price. Skip the auction house,
   keep them, or destroy them?
5. **Stacks**: compare prices per unit, and list in the stack sizes the buddy
   holds?
6. **Undercutting below the minimum**: a competing listing just above the
   minimum would be undercut below it. Stop at the minimum?
7. **Buddies undercutting each other**: prices slide down to the minimum and
   stay there. Acceptable, or skip undercutting other buddies' listings?
8. **Deposit and house cut**: both are higher at the neutral goblin auction
   houses. A buddy that can't afford the deposit vendors the item instead?
9. **Expired or sold auctions** come back by mail. Add a mailbox stop to the
   errand list (at which priority?) and relist returned items by the same rules?
10. **Owner leaves town mid-errand**: drop the list, or finish the current task
    first?
11. **Epics below level 60**: you said epics are always posted. Does that hold
    even when the item is already glutted (5+ listings)?

### User Request 11

I'm confused, the Isle of Quel Danas says it's items are leveled approximately
the same as a level 92 epic, and yet they sit at about the beginning of the
Naxxramas raid which scales from 83-92? The chart might have a subtle display
bug.

> 1. Soulbound items can't be auctioned or mailed. A bind-on-pickup level-60
> epic therefore can't be mailed to the owner. Should the buddy equip it if it's
> an upgrade and otherwise
     vendor it?

bind-on-pickup epics are all gained from dungeons or raids, I think. If so, they
should be tradeable to anyone that was there. Bots should need or greed
appropriately. Players and their bots essentially share a gear pool, with a
slight priority given to players for their BoE epics. Bots who have the profile
that splits their talent picks across all three tiers will always greed every
item they can equip (and which would be an upgrade) unless the average of their
clan (the player and their buddy bots) is above them - then they will need any
item that would be an upgrade, no matter which spec, until they are above the
average, then they greed again till they are below.

  2. "Level 60 or 60+ epic": by required level or by item level? A Molten Core
     epic requires 60 but has item level 66.

required level I think? Edge cases?

> 3. Only minimum-price listings present: you said "either list theirs or just
> vendor them". What decides between the two, a coin flip or list when there are
> fewer than 5?

for equipment they will vendor it. For trade goods they will list it. Can
playerbots do trade skills? If not, we should build out a system for that
eventually. For now, just let them list the extra trade goods.

> 4. Items with no vendor price have no minimum price. Skip the auction house,
> keep them, or destroy them?

we will have to go through them one-by-one. These items are rare, I don't mind
manually fiat'ing.

> 5. Stacks: compare prices per unit, and list in the stack sizes the buddy
> holds?

trade goods should be sold at random stacks just to mess with players. Well,
let's say they have 56 items that stack up to 20. They'd make random number
stacks between 1 and 20 unless the total that they have is 20 or fewer. If so,
then they post all of them.

> 6. Undercutting below the minimum: a competing listing just above the minimum
> would be undercut below it. Stop at the minimum?

Treat any listing that is close enough to the minimum to cause any undercut to
be below the minimum, as at the minimum. If they still need to post (for
example, to meet the 5 item quota) then their price should be their minimum
price even if it is more than the undercut would suggest, so long as it is still
below the price of the listing they're undercutting.

> 7. Buddies undercutting each other: prices slide down to the minimum and stay
> there. Acceptable, or skip undercutting other buddies' listings?

That's fine. If there's high demand, then prices will slide back up again. If
they have an expired listing that has no items left in the AH, they should
increase the price by 0.5x. This should repeat infinitely if necessary. This
will help prices slide up for rare goods that aren't posted often - or at least,
which didn't have any postings when they logged on to check.

> 8. Deposit and house cut: both are higher at the neutral goblin auction
> houses. A buddy that can't afford the deposit vendors the item instead?

A buddy that can't afford the deposit vendors in general, even if it's at a
regular auction house. They need the coin, and they need it now! Once they
attempt to auction something but can't, then they stop auctioning things and do
not return to the auction house until the next time they're in town. Or in a
different city of course.

> 9. Expired or sold auctions come back by mail. Add a mailbox stop to the
> errand list (at which priority?) and relist returned items by the same rules?

Oh yeah! Mailbox stops should be just below yellow item repair.

> 10. Owner leaves town mid-errand: drop the list, or finish the current task
> first?

finish the current task first.

> 11. Epics below level 60: you said epics are always posted. Does that hold
> even when the item is already glutted (5+ listings)?

yeah epics are always posted.

> - Do the rare item-level-100 epics that drop from dungeon trash also scale
> down? They sit above Naxxramas-40 by stat budget.

they are very rare. That's okay I think.

> - Some world blues also drop from dungeon trash, and a few may also drop from
> bosses. Should an item on both lists take the dungeon boss target?

they should be scaled according to how they are scaled in the world.

> - Besides the Shattrath portal, are there other ways onto the Isle to close,
> such as the Magisters' Terrace exit portal? I'll list them when building this.

Um. Not sure.

> - What replaces Capture Soul in the Fel Reaver's mechanics if it's removed
> from Kazzak?

Whatever it's replaced with in Kazzak's mechanics.

> Starting with the first edge case: when a buddy loots a bind-on-pickup
> level-60 epic it can't mail, should it equip it if it's an upgrade and
> otherwise vendor it?

if it's an upgrade, they probably rolled need for it. If so, yeah, they should
equip it of course. If it's one of the rare 3 spec characters, then if it's an
upgrade to any of the three specs that they are, they should equip it. This goes
for any item that enters their inventory - always equip upgrades.

## What I checked

- **Bind-on-pickup trading is already in the server.** Loot that binds on pickup
  stays tradeable for 2 hours to everyone who was there, so the shared gear pool
  needs nothing new there.
- **The bot module already rolls need or greed.** It needs an item that is
  usable and an upgrade, greeds otherwise, and can disenchant. Buddies follow
  that, plus your rule for three-way-split buddies.
- **Buddies have no trade skills of their own.** The bot module only crafts when
  its master tells it to. For now buddies list their surplus trade goods, and a
  buddy trade-skill system is noted for later.

## Recorded

- **Errands:** a mailbox stop, placed just below repairing yellow items. If the
  owner leaves town mid-errand, the buddy finishes its current task before
  following.
- **Auction pricing:** random stack sizes, the near-minimum rule, rising prices
  after an unsold expiry, and vendoring for the rest of the visit if the buddy
  can't afford a deposit. Equipment is vendored and trade goods are listed when
  only minimum-price listings exist. Epics are always posted. Items with no
  vendor price get a price list filled in by hand.
- **New buddy loot issue:** covers the shared gear pool, the three-way-split
  buddies rolling need or greed by the clan average, and always equipping
  upgrades from any source.
- **Rare item-level-100 epics:** stay as they are.
- **Blues in both the world and dungeons:** scaled the world way.
- **Capture Soul:** if Kazzak loses it, the Fel Reaver gets whatever replaces it
  on Kazzak.
- **Ways onto the Isle:** to be found when building, by listing every teleport
  whose destination is on the Isle and closing all of them except the Light's
  Hope flights.

## Open questions, most important first

1. **Best world blues:** keep them at ≈ 84, which touches the start of
   Naxxramas, or lower them to around 78–80?
2. **Rising auction prices:** "increase the price by 0.5x" could mean adding
   0.5× the vendor price each time (1.5× → 2× → 2.5×), or multiplying
   the last price by 1.5 (1.5× → 2.25× → 3.4×). Which?
3. **Mailing epics that require level 60+ has four edge cases:**
   - (a) On basic, every Outland item's required level is lowered to 60, so
     every Outland epic gets mailed and none reaches the auction house.
   - (b) Epics with no required level would always be auctioned.
   - (c) Epics that aren't gear (bags, recipes, mounts) fall under the same
     rule.
   - (d) A soulbound epic can't be mailed; I'd vendor it unless it's an upgrade.

   Rules for each?
4. **Clan average:** average equipped item level, or the bot module's own gear
   score?
5. **"A slight priority given to players for their BoE epics":** does that mean
   a player's Need beats a buddy's Need on a bind-on-equip epic, or that buddies
   pass when their owner could use it?
6. **Other players' buddies in a mixed group:** do all players and buddies
   present share one pool?

Starting with the first: should the best world blues touch the start of
Naxxramas (≈ 84), or sit lower at around 78–80?

### Assistant Response 12

> Now the level-display issue.

Kael'thas's epics are now set to item level 100 in the chart
(https://claude.ai/artifact/MZ1mumH84gVxVicSnskvD8), in the project copy, and in
the Outland dungeons issue. I've also opened a new issue for showing real levels

## Boss levels

You're right that they're out of order. Level is the only number a player sees
before a fight, so it should rise with the loot tier:

| Boss | Level now | Loot |
|---|---|---|
| Fel Reaver | 70 (stock) | epics 115–120 |
| Kazzak, Doomwalker | 73 (stock) | epics 120 |
| Kael'thas | 78 (proposed earlier) | epics 100 |

**Proposal:** all three world bosses at level 80, above every dungeon boss,
since they're one tier. Kael'thas drops to 77, one above the rest of Magisters'
Terrace. How hard each fight actually is will still come from the health and
damage multipliers.

## Showing the real level instead of the skull

The server already sends the real level; it's the game client that hides it. The
client reports the level as unknown for anything 10 or more levels above you,
and for every creature ranked as a boss. The interface then draws a skull in the
target frame, focus frame, tooltip and nameplates. No server patch alone can
change that.

- **Recommended: an interface addon.** The project already runs AIO, the channel
  that lets the server feed data to client addons. When you target, focus or
  mouse over a creature, the addon asks the server for its level and draws the
  number where the skull would be. It reaches players through the project's
  addon install or through AIO itself.
- **Alternative: patching the game program** to raise the 10-level limit. That
  means handing every player a modified executable, and I haven't checked
  whether a known patch exists for this build.

## Recorded

- **Best world blues:** they stay at ≈ 84.
- **Rising prices:** after each unsold expiry, the price goes up by another
  0.5× the vendor price, with no ceiling, until the item sells.
- **Epics:**
  - An upgrade is equipped.
  - A bind-on-equip epic that isn't an upgrade is kept until the buddy reaches a
    mailbox, then mailed to its owner. It is never sold or vendored.
  - A soulbound epic that isn't an upgrade is vendored.
  - Epics that aren't gear, and epics with no required level, are auctioned.
- **Clan average:** average equipped item level.

About your "For what?": I meant loot rolls in a group of two players plus their
buddies. My proposal is that each three-way-split buddy compares itself only
with its own clan's average, and rolls between the two clans are ordinary group
rolls.

## Open questions

1. Put the world bosses at 80 and Kael'thas at 77?
2. Should boss-ranked creatures (Kazzak, dungeon end bosses) also show their
   number, or keep the skull as a "this is a boss" sign?
3. Is the interface addon the route you want for showing levels?
4. When an item finally sells after its price has climbed, does the next copy
   start from that sold price or back at the minimum?
5. Should buddies that follow one spec use the bot module's stock rolls: need on
   an upgrade for their spec, greed otherwise?
6. For mixed groups: clans stay separate, and rolls between them are normal.
   Agreed?

Starting with the first: world bosses at 80 and Kael'thas at 77?

    and protection's at 1/3; a buddy split into thirds takes a third of each.

### Assistant Response 15

Yes, it can be done. I've written it into the Outland world gear issue as a
proposal; nothing is built.

**How it would work:**
- **Bind on pickup:** each item has a binding setting in the world database.
  Setting it to "binds when picked up" for every Outland blue weapon and armor
  piece covers the open world, the Isle of Quel'Danas and dungeon trash. Most
  dungeon boss blues probably bind on pickup already; I'll check them when
  building.
- **The 2-hour trade window already exists in the server.** When a
  bind-on-pickup item drops, it can be traded for 2 hours of played time. This
  is controlled by the setting `Item.SetItemTradeable`, which is on by default.
- **The catch:** the stock window only covers group members who could loot that
  corpse. A buddy that wasn't grouped with you, or wasn't nearby, wouldn't
  qualify. So at loot time a small rule in basic's compiled rules file would add
  the looter's whole clan (the owner and all their buddies) to the list,
  wherever they are.

**What changes as a result:**
- No Outland blue can reach the auction house, from players or buddies. The
  buddies' blue auction rules would then only cover blues from Azeroth.
- A player can't hand an Outland blue to a friend outside their clan, even
  within the 2 hours.

**Questions:**
1. Should it work both ways within the clan? A blue a buddy loots would also be
   tradeable to its owner for 2 hours, not only player → buddies.
2. The rare item-level-100 world epics: keep them bind-on-equip, so buddies mail
   them to their owner, or make them bind on pickup like the blues?

### Assistant Response 16

I've recorded your two answers and opened a new issue for removing tradeskills
from Outland. Nothing is built yet.

**Clan trading** (in the Outland gear issue and the buddy loot issue): a buddy
holding an Outland blue it can't use scores it for everyone in the clan. It uses
each member's stat weights, the table of how much each stat is worth to their
spec. For the owner it scores the item once per talent tree of the owner's
class, because it can't know which spec the owner plays, and takes the best
result. It trades the blue to whoever gains most, or vendors it if nobody gains.
The item-level-100 world epics stay bind-on-equip.

### Assistant Response 17

> Writing the answers into the tradeskills issue and opening one for
> Inscription.

I've recorded all of it and opened a new issue for Inscription without glyphs.
Nothing is built yet.

## Fishing and cooking

Yes to keeping both, with one edge case. Every Outland zone has a fishing skill
below which most casts come up empty:

| Zone | Fishing skill needed |
|---|---|
| Hellfire Peninsula, Shadowmoon Valley | 280 |
| Zangarmarsh | 305 |
| Terokkar Forest, Isle of Quel'Danas | 355 |
| Nagrand, Netherstorm | 380 |

With professions capped at 300, only two zones' pools would be worth fishing.
Cooking Outland fish and meat also needs more than 300.

**Proposal:** fishing and cooking keep their full range to 375, Master training
and recipes included, as the one exception to the cap. Both are self-contained
(fish from pools, meat from creatures) and make food for buddies.

A related question is First Aid. Netherweave bandages need more than 300 First
Aid. Keeping First Aid to 375 as well would give Netherweave Cloth a use.

## Inscription without glyphs

Of its 449 recipes, 345 are glyphs. Up to 300, what's left is:

- **Milling and 13 inks.** Most inks exist to feed glyphs.
- **Stat scrolls (Stamina, Intellect, Spirit, Agility, Strength), ranks I–V.**
  Stamina VI is at 300; the other rank VIs are at 305–320, past the cap. At
  rank V the buffs are Stamina +34, Spirit +26, Intellect +21, Agility +15 and
  Strength +15. At rank VI they're +43, +32, +24, +20 and +20. Scroll of
  Protection (armor) is a drop, not crafted.
- **Scroll of Recall I and II**, a teleport scroll.
- **Armor and Weapon Vellum I and II.** Enchanters put an enchantment on a
  vellum so it can be sold or traded.
- **Nine off-hand books.**
- **Four Tarots and a Darkmoon Card.**
- **The daily glyph-research recipe**, which becomes pointless and goes.

**The problem for a buff-scroll profession is stacking.** In the server's
spell-stacking rules:
- **Only one scroll can be active on a character at a time**, whatever its stat.
- **Each scroll competes with the class buff for the same stat, and only the
  stronger one stays.** For example, a Scroll of Stamina competes with Power
  Word: Fortitude.
- **Class buffs at 60 are bigger than these scrolls**, so a buddy who casts the
  matching buff makes the scroll worthless. With buddies around, scrolls would
  be worth less than today, not more.

## Also recorded

- **Clan trading:** when a buddy gives an item to its owner and another buddy
  also wanted it, it says so in chat ("hey, so-and-so was also interested in
  this item...").
- **Outland tradeskills:** gas clouds and skinning are removed. Netherweave
  Cloth still drops but has no use, and motes stay as vendor items; the
  mote-exchange quest idea is noted for later. Recipes above 300 are removed
  from drops and vendors, and Jewelcrafting goes to 300.

## Questions

1. Keep fishing and cooking to 375? And First Aid too?
2. Should scrolls of different stats stack with each other? And with class
   buffs, or does the stronger one still win there?
3. Make rank VI of every scroll learnable at 300, or raise the scroll values?
4. Keep the Tarots, the Darkmoon Card and the off-hand books, or trim
   Inscription to scrolls, inks, vellums and Recall?

### Assistant Response 18

> Replacing "owner" with your name wherever the issue files quote you.

Sorry about that, Ritz, and yes, I'd like that. Wherever the basic-profile,
buddy and accuracy-cap issue files quote your decisions, they now say "Ritz"
instead of "owner". The buddy issues still use "owner" for the player character
a buddy belongs to; say if you'd prefer "their player" there too.

## Scaling all eight scroll ranks

The stock values, from the client's spell file:

| Rank (required level) | Stamina | Intellect | Spirit | Agility / Strength | Protection (armor) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| V (50) | 34 | 21 | 26 | 15 | 240 |
| VI (60) | 43 | 24 | 32 | 20 | 285 |
| VII (70) | 63 | 32 | 40 | 25 | 340 |
| VIII (80) | 132 | 48 | 64 | 30 | 750 |

The proposal, following the same idea as the item-level scaling:
- **Required levels × 0.75:** I 1, II 11, III 23, IV 30, V 38, VI 45, VII 53,
  VIII 60.
- **Skill needed × 300/425:** rank VIII's 405–425 becomes about 290–300.
- **Values, pick one:**
  - **(A) Keep the stock values.** Rank VIII at 60 would give +132 Stamina and
    +750 armor, on top of the class buff. That's stronger than most class buffs.
  - **(B) Rank VIII gets stock rank VI's values**, the ones Blizzard sized for
    level 60: +43, +24, +32, +20, +285. The lower ranks spread out below it.
  - **(C) Rank VIII gets stock rank VII's values:** +63, +32, +40, +25, +340.

I'd take **(B)**. Now that scrolls stack with class buffs, level-60 values are
already a real gain, and (A) would make one scroll matter more than a whole
buff.

## Stacking and the timer

Yes. Your rule needs only one change in the server's stacking table, and the
one-at-a-time rule already exists:
- **Stacks with class buffs:** stock scrolls sit in "the stronger one wins"
  groups with class buffs, for example a Stamina scroll with Power Word:
  Fortitude. Taking the scrolls out of those groups makes them stack.
- **One scroll per character:** the "Scrolls" group already allows only one
  active at a time. It stays as it is.
- **Timer:** scrolls last 30 minutes now. Each scroll's duration can be changed
  through the spell-corrections patch. How long should they last?

## Vellums

The ranks differ in which enchantments they can hold:
- **I:** only enchantments with no level restriction.
- **II:** enchantments restricted to level 35 or lower.
- **III:** enchantments restricted to level 60 or lower. It needs skill 355
  (armor) or 405 (weapon), which is past basic's cap.

Enchanters at 60 need rank III for most of their enchantments. I suggest making
it learnable at 300.

## Tarots

Each tarot gives a random card from one deck. A full deck is handed in at the
Darkmoon Faire for a reward:

| Tarot | Deck | Reward |
|---|---|---|
| Mysterious | Rogues | level-10 chest armor (item level 20) |
| Strange | Swords | level-20 shoulders (item level 25) |
| Arcane | Mages | level-30 necklaces (item level 35) |
| Shadowy | Demons | level-40 weapons (item level 45) |

The Darkmoon Card at skill 290 draws from a higher deck; I didn't check which.
These are collectibles for levelling characters. They only work if the Darkmoon
Faire runs on basic.

## Fishing, cooking and First Aid to 375

Keeping these three to 375 means keeping 20 cooking, 4 First Aid and 1 fishing
training entries above 300.

**Fishing skill needed per zone:**
- Hellfire Peninsula and Shadowmoon Valley: 280.
- Zangarmarsh: 305.
- Terokkar Forest and the Isle of Quel'Danas: 355.
- Nagrand and Netherstorm: 380.

**Food.** Only one "well fed" buff can be active at a time.

| Best Azeroth food | Buff |
|---|---|
| Dirge's Kickin' Chimaerok Chops | +25 Stamina |
| Smoked Desert Dumplings | +20 Strength |
| Monster Omelet | +12 Stamina and +12 Spirit |
| Other foods | +10 Agility, +10 Intellect, or +10 mana every 5 seconds |

| Outland food | Buff (all also give +20 Spirit) |
|---|---|
| Fisherman's Feast, Spicy Crawdad | +30 Stamina |
| Warp Burger, Grilled Mudfish | +20 Agility |
| Roasted Clefthoof | +20 Strength |
| Blackened Basilisk, Golden Fish Sticks | +23 spell power |
| Spicy Hot Talbuk | +20 hit rating (2% hit at level 60) |
| Skullfish Soup | +20 crit rating |

Outland food is roughly twice as strong as Azeroth's.

**Bandages** (healing over 8 seconds):

| Bandage | Heals |
|---|---|
| Runecloth | 1,360 |
| Heavy Runecloth (best at 300) | 2,000 |
| Netherweave | 2,800 |
| Heavy Netherweave | 3,400 |

## Questions

1. Scroll values: (A), (B) or (C)?
2. Scroll duration: keep 30 minutes, or shorter?
3. Make Vellum III learnable at 300?
4. Keep fishing, cooking and First Aid to 375, now that you've seen the numbers?
